                                                                                                                                                 Ill
             Preface

             This publication describes the components of the IBM 7690 Clinical
             Workstation (Type 7690) and their interactions.

             The information in this publication is for reference and is intended to
             be used by hardware and software designers, programmers, engi-
             neers, and others who need to understand the design and operation
             of the Type 7690.

             This manual is organized as follows:
              • Section 1, "System Board/Interface Adapter" describes the func-
                tions provided by the system board and interface adapter.
              • Section 2, "Power Supply" provides electrical input/output spec-
                ifications as well as operational information about the power
                supply.
              • Section 3, "Keyboard" describes the hardware, function,
                encoding, and layout of the keyboard.
              • Section 4, "Bar Code Feature" provides a description of the bar
                code feature's hardware and details its programmable options.
              • Section 5, "Touch Panel" describes the programming interface
                to the touch panel's device driver (LEDTOUCH.SYS).
     E        • Section 6, "System BIOS" describes the basic input/output
                system (BIOS) and the interrupt interfaces. This section also con-
     E   •      tains a BIOS memory map, descriptions of vectors with special
                meanings, and a set of low memory maps.
              • Section 7, "Programming Tips and Techniques" contains
                pointers that should be considered when developing software for
                the Type 7690.

             An Index is also provided.

                                                                                                                                      System Board/Interface Adapter   1-5
    1-4      System Board/Interface Adapter
The microprocessor is supported by two high-function support
                                                                          System Clock Generator
devices: a system support gate array and an 1/0 support gate array.

                                                                          T~~ system _clock generator uses a 48 MHz input that is internally
System Support Gate Array                                                 drvrded to grve the output clock signal of 8 MHz with a 33% duty
                                                                          cycle. It also generates a 1.84 MHz signal for the serial port.
The system support gate array contains the bus controller, the
memory controller and parity checker, the wait-state generator and        !he clock ge,n~rator generates the 'reset' signal after sensing the
bus conversion logic, the system clock generator, and the OMA page         power good signal from the power supply.
register and support logic.

Bus Controller

The Type 7690 has two bus masters on the local bus: the micro-
processor, and the system support gate array. The gate array seizes
the bus to generate memory refresh and OMA bus cycles. It controls
the request/grant line (CPU RO/GT) , which is connected to the micro-
processor.

The gate array generates a request pulse to the microprocessor to
get control of the bus. The microprocessor then gives control of the
bus and pulses the same line to indicate a grant.

Memory Controller and Parity Checker

The memory controller functions of the gate array control memory
and generate the memory refresh . Memory must be refreshed once
every 4 ms. Memory refresh takes nine clock cycles of 125 ns
through a dedicated refresh channel within the gate array.

The parity checker function generates the parity bits for system
memory and activates the '-parity check' signal when a parity error is
detected. Only the read/write memory on the system board is
checked.

Bus Conversion Logic and Walt-State Generator

The bus conversion logic converts word transfers to 1/0 devices into
2-byte transfers. Sixteen-bit transfers are only supported for the sys-
tem's read-only and read/write memory.

Additional logic generates the needed wait states for the micro-
processor bus cycles to 1/0 devices. This logic monitors the '1/0 CH
ROY' line to determine the wait states required.

Interrupt Sharing
                                                                                    Program Support
A standardized hardware design concept has been established to
enable multiple adapters to share an interrupt level. The integrated                The interrupt-sharing program support described in the following pro-
adapters do not use interrupt sharing. The following describes th is                vides for an orderly means to :
design concept and discusses the programming support requi red.
                                                                                     •   Link a task's interrupt handler to a chain of interrupt handlers
Design Overview                                                                      •   Share the interrupt level while the task is acti ve

Most interrupt supporting adapters hold the IRQ line inactive and then               •   Unlink the interrupt handler from the chain when the task is deac-
drive the line active to cause an interrupt. In contrast, the shared                     tivated.
interrupt hardware design allows the IRQ line to float high. Each
adapter on the line may cause an interrupt by pulsing the line low.
The leading edge of the pulse arms the interrupt cont roller; the
trailing edge of the pulse causes the interrupt.

ROM

The system board has 64K by 8-bits of ROM or erasable program-
mable read-only memory (EPROM). Two module sockets are pro-
vided ; both sockets have 32K by 8-bits of ROM . This ROM contains
POST, BIOS, dot patterns for 128 characters in graph ics mode, and a
diskette bootstrap loader. The ROM is packaged in 28-pin modules.

The interface adapter contains 16K by 8-bits of ROM , which contains
the POST and BIOS routines for the LCD, touch panel, and bar code
feature.

Palette Read Address Register, 3C7: This register contains the             MCGA to LCD Conversion
pointer to one of 256 palette data registers and is used when reading
the color palette.                                                         There are several special requirements in order to convert the MCGA
                                                                           data into LCD data. The following is a summary of some of the con-
Reading this port returns the last command cycle to the palette. The       siderations.
description of bits 1 and O is in the following table. All other bits
during a read of this port are reserved.                                   The LCD panel requires that two halves of the display be drawn at the
                                                                           same time. Because the MCGA data is serial (the two halves come
  Bit 1         BitO     Last Palette Command                              out at separate times), half of the data must be stored and later
                                                                           retrieved when the remainder of the image is available. This split
   0          0          Write Palette Cycle                               frame control requires two 32K x 8 RAMs to completely store the
   0          1          Reserved
   1          0          Reserved
                                                                           image during this process.
                         Read Palette Cycle

Figure          1-49. Last Palette Command

Color Palette Address Register, 3C8: This register contains the
pointer to one of 256 palette data registers and is used during a
palette load .

Color Palette Data Register, 3C9: This register contains a 6-bit value
that yields one of 64 color levels. To write a color, the address is
loaded into the Color Palette Address register. Three writes to this
register are needed for each palette address: the first is the red color
information, the second is the green, and the third is the blue.

To read a color, the address value is written to the Palette Read
Address register, followed by three reads of this register. The first
returns the red color information, the second returns the green, and
the third returns the blue.

          7            Not Used
          6            Not Used
          5            PD 5

          2            PD 2
          1            PD 1
          0            PDO

The following programming example uses the BIOS routine to load a
font table into block 0. Because of differences in the hardware, the
character generator is not loaded the same for all display adapters;
however, the BIOS routines are the same for all video subsystems
with RAM-loadable fonts. The Type 7690, for instance, supports only
8-by-8 and 8-by-16 character fonts, depending on the mode selected.

Programming Considerations

Interrupt Usage: The Type 7690 video subsystem can be programmed
to create an interrupt at the end of each vertical display refresh time.
An interrupt handler must be written by the application to take advan-
tage of this feature. The vertical retrace interrupt is on 1Ra2. (This
interrupt does not support interrupt sharing) .

The programmer can poll the Interrupt Control register, port 3D5
index 11, to determine whether the video caused the interrupt. The
IRQ2 status bit indicates that a vertical retrace interrupt did occur; it
does not indicate that the video is still in retrace . To find the status of
the 'vertical retrace' signal, check the CGA Status register, port 3DA.

The Interrupt Control register also has 2 bits that control the interrupt
circuitry and 1 bit that controls the output of the video formatter. To
enable the interrupt:
 1. Clear bit 4 to clear the interrupt latch.
 2. Clear bit 5 to enable the interrupt.
 3. Set bit 4 to enable the latch.

512 Character Set: When using a 512 character set on the Type 7690,
the following procedures are recommended to maintain consistent
colors.
 1. Set the block specifier, (AX)= 1103H.
 2. Set the colors for 512, (AX)= 1000H (BX)= 0712H.
 3. Reload the first eight colors into the palette.
Note: The character hex 20 (normally a space) is used to fill the blank
area of the screen. Therefore, it is recommended that character hex
20 be a blank space.

ND           Nondata Mode-This indicates an operation in the
             nondata mode.
PCN          Present Cylinder Number-The cylinder number at the
             completion of a Sense Interrupt Status command
             (present position of the head).
R            Record-The sector number to be read or written.
SC           Sector-The number of sectors per cylinder.
SK           Skip-The skip deleted-data address mark.

1-102   Diskette Drive
                                                                                                                             Diskette Drive   1-103
Signal Description                                                         The following are the output signals from the diskette drive. These
                                                                           signal thresholds are +3.7 Vdc high and +0.4 Vdc low.
All signals are 74HCT series-compatible in both rise and fall times
and in interface levels. The following are the input signals to the        -Index: An active pulse of 1 ms indicates the diskette index.
diskette drive. These signal thresholds are + 2.0 Vdc high and + 0.8
Vdc low.                                                                   -Track 0: When this signal is active, the head is on track 0. This
                                                                           signal is used to determine whether the drive is present. If, after
-Drive Select 0-1: The select lines provide the means to enable or         commanding the drive to seek track 0, the '-track 0' signal does not
disable the drive interface lines. When the signal is active, the drive    go active, the drive is not present.
is enabled. When the signal is inactive, all control inputs are ignored,
and the drive outputs are disabled. The maximum drive-select delay         -Write Protect: This signal is active when the write-protect window is
time is 500 ns.                                                            uncovered. When this happens, the write current circuits are disa-
                                                                           bled .
-Motor Enable 0-1: When this signal is made active, the spindle
starts to turn. When it is made inactive, the spindle slows to a stop.     -Read Data: An active pulse on this line writes a logical 1. The pulse
                                                                           width for the 250,000-bps rate is 250 ns.
-Step: An active pulse on this line causes the head to move one
track. The minimum pulse width is 1 µs. The direction of the head          -Diskette Change: This signal is active at power-on and whenever the
motion is determined by the state of the '-direction ' signal at the       diskette is removed. It remains active until a diskette is present and
trail ing edge of the '-step' pulse.                                       a '-step' pulse is received.

-Direction: When this signal is active, the head moves to the next
higher track (toward the spindle) for each '-step ' pulse. When the
signal is inactive, the head moves toward track 0. This signal must
be stable for 1 µs before and after the trailing edge of the '-step '
pulse.

-Head 1 Select: When this signal is active, the upper head (head 1) is
selected. When it is inactive, the lower head {head 0) is selected.

-Write Enable: When this signal is active, the write-current circuits
are enabled and data can be written under the control of the '-write
data' signal. Th is signal must be active 8 µs before data can be
written .

-Write Data: An active pulse on this line writes a 1. These pulses
have a 4-, 6-, or 8-µs spacing with a width of 250 ns for the
250,Q00-bps transfer rate. Write precompensation of 125 ns is done
by the diskette gate array.

                                                                      The serial port has a controller that provides the following functions:
                                                                       • Adds or deletes standard, asynchronous communications bits to
                                                                         or from a serial data stream .

                                                                       •   Provides full double buffering, which eliminates the need for
                                                                           precise synchronization.

                                                                       •   Provides a programmable baud-rate generator.
                                                                       •   Provides modem controls (CTS, RTS, DSR, DTR, RI, and CD).

Beeper

The beeper and its control circuits and driver are on the system
board. The beeper drive circuit is capable of approximately 1/2 watt
of power. The control circuits allow the beeper to be driven three dif-
ferent ways:

 1. A Direct Program Control register bit may be toggled to generate
    a pulse train.
 2. The clock input to the timer can be modulated with a program-                          40
                                                                                                39
                                                                                                                               t
    controlled 1/0 port bit.
 3. The output from channel 2 of the timer may be programmed to                                                   J6
                                                                                                 J12
    generate a waveform to the beeper.

 Channel 2     (Tone generation for beeper)                                                2
                                                                                                                       .       12

                                                                            •                                          A40

               Used to drive beeper
                                                                            I

All three methods may be performed simultaneously. For more infor-
mation, see "System Timer " and "1/0 Ports" earlier in this section.



Earphone, Connector

An earphone connector is provided at the rear of the system unit to
allow the user to disable the internal beeper (speaker) and listen to
the sound output through a set of earphones. The internal beeper is              Ref.#     Description                 Ref. #       Description
disabled whenever a plug is inserted into this connector.
                                                                                 1         Serial Connector             8           Earphone Connector
                                                                                 2         Parallel Connector          9            80-pin 1/0 Connector
The connector will accept any 1/4-inch diameter audio plug. A                    3         Diskette Drive Connector    10           Fan Connector
monophonic earphone with an impedance level of 15 to 35 ohms is                  4         Microprocessor              11           Power Supply Connector
                                                                                 5         Additional Memory (128K)    12           Display Connector
recommended . Some earphones with an impedance level as high as
                                                                                 6         (Reserved)                  13           Memory SIPS (512K)
100 ohms may also be acceptable,. The drive level is fixed so that the           7         Keyboard Connector
sound level will be directly related to the sensitivity of the earphones.
Earphones with 1/8-inch plugs may be used with adapter plugs.                   Figure   1-117. System Board Connector Location

1-130   System Board
                                                                                                   Power Supply       2-1
Description                                                               Input and Output Power
The system de power supply is a single-phase, 60-watt, five-voltage
                                                                          Tl he_ nominal power requirements and voltages are listed in the fol-
level supply. It is internal to the system unit and supplies power for     owing tables.
the system board, the LCD, the option adapters, the keyboard, and
the battery pack. The supply provides 7.0 A of + 5 Vdc, 1.6 A of + 12      Nominal (Vac)           Minimum (Vac)      Maximum (Vac)   Maximum Current
Vdc, 300 mA of -12 Vdc, and 70 mA of -22.3 Vdc to the system board.
All power levels to the system board are monitored with undervoltage         115                   90                 137             2.0A
and overcurrent protection.
                                                                         Figure       2-1. Vac Input Requirements
The power supply input is protected by an external fuse. If de over-
current or undervoltage conditions exist, the supply automatically
                                                                          Nominal          Load Current (A)
shuts down until the condition is corrected.                                                                       Regulation          Ripple
                                                                          (Vdc)            Min       Max           Tolerance           (mV p-p)
The system board and storage devices take approximately 4 A of +5          +5Vdc           1.5          7.0        +5% to -3%          100
Vdc, thus allowing approximately 3 A of + 5 Vdc for the adapters in        + 12 Vdc        0.12         1.7        +5% to -3%          120
                                                                          -12 Vdc          0.015
the system expansion slots. The + 12 Vdc power level is designed to                                     0.25       + 10% to-8%         120
power the internal diskette drive. The + 12 Vdc and -12 Vdc are used     Figure    2-2. Vdc Output
for powering the EIA drivers for the serial port. All three power
levels are bussed across the four system expansion slots.

The power supply provides a constant current of 100 mA to the
battery pack, which consists of seven Ni-Cad, 2.2 Ampere-hour cells.
When properly charged, the battery pack provides 3 minutes of
backup power for the keyboard and memory. The LCD, touch panel,
and diskette drive are inoperative while the system is being powered
by the battery pack. The battery pack becomes the active power
source whenever primary power is lost while the power switch is in
the ON position.

       Keyboard ID                   AB84
                                                                        tinues scanning and outputs the two ID bytes, low byte (Hex AB) first,
Figure    3-2. Commands from the Keyboard                               then high byte (Hex 84) . Following the output of the ID bytes, the key-
                                                                        board commences scanning.
BAT Completion Code (Hex AA): Following satisfactory completion of
the BAT, the keyboard sends hex AA. Any other code indicates a
failure of the keyboard.

BAT Failure Code (Hex FC): If a BAT failure occurs, the keyboard
sends this code, discontinues scanning, and waits for a system
response or reset.

Key Detection Error (Hex FF): The keyboard sends a key detection
error character (hex FF) if conditions in the keyboard make it impos-
sible to identify a switch closure.

Overrun (Hex FF): An overrun character (hex FF) is placed in the key-
board buffer and replaces the last code when the buffer capacity has
been exceeded. The code is sent to the system when it reaches the
top of the buffer queue.

Resend (Hex FE): The keyboard issues a RESEND command fol-
lowing the receipt of an invalid input or any input with incorrect
parity. If the system has not sent anything to the keyboard , no
response is required.

ACK (Hex FA): The keyboard issues an ACK response to any valid
input other than ECHO or RESEND. If the keyboard is interrupted
during the output of an ACK, it will abort the ACK and process the
new command.

3-28   Keyboard                                                                Bar Code Feature        4-1
Description
                                                                            Bar Code Controller
The Type 7690 supports a bar code wand/card reader feature. The
bar code wand is connected to a bar code controller which in turn is        The bar code controller is a 40 pin IC combined with a 1Kx8 RAM. It
connected to the keyboard micro-controller.
                                                                            can decode several types of bar codes and output the decoded data
                                                                            over a full-duplex serial port to the keyboard micro-controller.
The bar code controller is responsible for the actual bar code scan-
ning and the conversion of bar code data to ASCII. The ASCII bar
                                                                           The narrowest bar must generate a pulse width greater than 150 µSec
code data is then sent to the keyboard micro-controller where it is        and the widest bar must generate a pulse width less than 70 MSec.
converted to the appropriate scan codes, serialized, and merged into       The bar code must have a minimum contrast ratio of 45% and be
the keyboard data stream for transmission to the system board. See         scanned with a tilt angle between vertical and 45 degrees.
" Bar Code Characters" on page 3-24 for scan code information.

A single carriage return (0Dh) follows the bar code data by default.
                                                                           Bar Code Types
To distinguish bar code data from normal keyboard data, application
programs can request that a header and/or trailer be appended to the       The bar code controller can automatically recognize and correctly
bar code data. Refer to "Specifying Special Bar Code Data Formats "        decode the following bar codes:
on page 4-12 for information about bar code headers and trailers.           •   Code 39 (ANSI MH10.8M-1983, MIL-STD-1189)
                                                                            •   Interleaved 2 of 5 Code (ANSI MH10.8M-1983)
                                                                            •   UPC/EAN/JAN Codes
Electrical Characteristics                                                  •   Standard Codabar (NM7)
                                                                            •   Code 128
The bar code reader uses a single TTL input bit. A high level (1) indi-
cates a dark bar while a low level (0) indicates a light bar. All bar     Any of these codes can be scanned bidirectionally. Supplemental
width detection and decode algorithms are performed by the bar code       digits can also be read but the code must be scanned in the forward
controller.                                                               direction; the supplemental digits must be scanned last. The fol-

The touch panel employed by the Type 7690 is a low resolution                                          Touch Panel Not Installed
                                                                                       6               -Sel Data All High
pointing device that can resolve a touch point to a single column/row                  5               Keyboard Not Installed
coordinate. A maximum of 79 discreet points can be detected in the                     4               Reserved
horizontal plane, and a maximum of 47 discreet points can be                           3               Scanner Type (1 = Wand , 0=Scanner)
                                                                                       2               ADC Check (Input over 1/2 scale)
detected in the vertical plane.
                                                                                                       LCD Power OK (-22V)
                                                                                       0               + 12V OK (-Battery Power/Fuse Open)
The touch panel is based on an infrared technology: it uses light emit-
ting diodes (LEDs) and light sensitive detectors that are mounted          Figure           5-2. F301 h Diagnostic Data
behind the black translucent bezel that borders the LCD. Each LED in
the horizontal and vertical plane is aligned with a corresponding light
detector at the opposite side of the LCD. A touch point is detected                 Bit        1/0    Signal Name
when a horizontal and vertical light beam is interrupted.
                                                                                    7          0      Reserved
                                                                                    6          0      Reserved
The smallest object that can be detected by the touch panel is equal                5          0      Reserved
to the distance between the outside edges of two adjacent LEDs, as                  4          0      Reserved
shown below.                                                                       3           1/0    + ADC Convert Busy/ + Start ADC

5-18   Touch Panel                                                                   System BIOS       6-1
                                                                           If a BIOS function has several possible operations, the AH register is
System BIOS Usage                                                          used as input to indicate the desired operation. For example, to set
                                                                           the time of day, the following code is required:
The basic input/output system (BIOS) resides in ROM on the system
board and provides device-level control for the major 1/0 devices in       MOV   AH,1           ;Function is to set time of day
                                                                           MOV   CX,HIGH COUNT ;Establish the current time
the system. Additional ROM modules may be located on adapters to           MOV   DX,LOW COUNT
provide device-level control for that adapter. BIOS routines enable        INT   lAH -          ;Set the time
the assembler language programmer to perform block or character-
level 1/0 operations without concern for device address and operating      To read the time of day:
characteristics. System services, such as memory size determi-
                                                                           MOV AH,0             ;Function is to read time of day
nation, are provided by the BIOS.                                          INT lAH              ;Read the timer

The goal of BIOS is to provide an operational interface to the system
                                                                          Generally, the BIOS routines save all registers except for AX and the
and relieve the programmer of the concern about the characteristics       flags. Other registers are modified on return only if they are
of hardware devices. The BIOS interface insulates the user from the       returning a value to the caller. The exact register use is in the
hardware, allowing new devices to be added to the system, yet             description of each BIOS function .
retaining the BIOS level interface to the device. In this manner, hard-
ware modifications and enhancements are transparent to user pro-
grams.
                                                                          Hardware Interrupts
The IBM Personal Computer Macro Assembler manual and the IBM
Personal Computer Disk Operating System (DOS) manual provide              For the hardware interrupt assignments, see the Software Interrupt
useful programming information related to this section. A description     Listing table later in this section. Interrupt level 0 corresponds to
of the BIOS interface is given in this section.                           interrupt vector 8, level 1 to interrupt vector 9, and so forth, including
                                                                          interrupt level 7, which corresponds to interrupt vector OF.
Access to BIOS is through the 8086 software interrupts. The software
interrupts hex 10 through 1A each access a different BIOS routine.        For information about sharing interrupts, see " Interrupt Sharing " in
For example, to determine the amount of memory available in the           Section 1.
system,

          INT 12H                                                         Software Interrupts

invokes the BIOS routine for determining memory size and returns          With software interrupt sharing , it is possible for software interrupt
the value to the caller.                                                  routines to "daisy chain " BIOS interrupts hex 10 through 1F, similar
                                                                          to hardware interrupt routines. The interrupt routine must check the
Parameter Passing                                                         function value in AH, and if the value is not in the routine' s range of
                                                                          function calls, the interrupt routine transfers control to the next
All parameters passed to and from the BIOS routines go through the        routine in the chain.
microprocessor registers. The description of each BIOS function
shows the registers used on the Call and the Return. For the memory
size example, no parameters are passed. The memory size, in 1K
increments, is returned in the AX register.

  out of synchronization, or damage to the gate array will result.                 be 0 for the module to be deemed valid.

text modes and three of the graphics modes are standard CGA                       application requires that different cursor types be preserved for
modes.                                                                            different pages, it must maintain the different types itself.

Color Mapping Considerations                                                      Type 7690 and a color system.
                                                                               3. Strategic attribute selection
Since the LCD is a black and white display, application programs for
the Type 7690 should only use black and white display attributes. The             The LCD interface is designed so that all EVEN colors appear
Type 7690 automatically remaps color video modes to their black and               black and all ODD colors appear white on the Type 7690's LCD. A
white equivalents to maintain a level of compatibility with existing              strategic color application would use opposing foreground and
color applications.                                                               background colors (ODD on EVEN or EVEN on ODD) that produce
                                                                                  pleasing color combinations on a color system while producing
When referring to sections in this manual pertaining to video modes,              visible characters on the Type 7690's LCD.
keep in mind that the modes are remapped as follows:
                                                                                   Even Colors                        Odd Colors

         Mode         Mode                                                         00     Black                       01   Blue
         Requested    Enabled                                                      02     Green                       03   Cyan
                                                                                   04     Red                         05   Magenta
        0             0                                                            06     Brown                       07   White
        1             0                                                            08     Gray                        09   Light Blue
        2             2                                                            OA     Light Green                 OB   Light Cyan
        3             2                                                            oc     Light Red                   OD   Light Magenta
        4             4                                                            OE     Light Yellow                OF   Bright White
        5             4
        6             6

Figure       7-1. Video Mode Map
                                                                              The Type 7690 BIOS contains a color map function that can be
Note: Even though video mode 13 is supported, its implementation is           enabled or disabled by an application program. Refer to " BIOS Data
not recommended due to the color limitations of the LCD.                      Area and Locations" on page 6-60 (Address 40:89). This function is
                                                                              enabled by default. When enabled, the color map function ensures
Selecting Display Attributes                                                  that text written through Int 10H (AH= 09), with different foreground
                                                                              and background color combinations, is visible on the LCD.
There are three methods for selecting display attributes on the Type
7690:                                                                         If the application program specifies (through BIOS) an odd foreground
                                                                              color on an odd background color, the color map function changes the
 1. Attribute selection for the Type 7690 only                                foreground to an even color. If the application specifies an even fore-
      If the application is intended only to run on the Type 7690, choose     ground color on an even background color, the color map function
      only black and white attributes (color O for black, color 7 for         changes the foreground to an odd color. For example, if the applica-
      white).                                                                 tion specifies white on blue, color map changes the colors to brown
                                                                              on blue (which would appear as black on white on the LCD).
 2. Attribute selection based on video modes
                                                                              Note: Applications that write to the screen directly (bypassing BIOS)
      If the video mode is 0, 2, 4, or 6 use black and white attributes. If   cannot take advantage of the color map function.
      the video mode is 1, 3, or 5 use any of the color attribute combi-
      nations.

 Sensing a Power Loss Condition                                           Blanking the LCD

Since the diskette drive is not operational under battery power, the
application should provide for unlimited retries during diskette 1/0      Power On Indicator
operations when primary power is lost.
